2. A.     05.06.14 
2. RECOGNITION 
A.  2013-2014 Outstanding Support Staff Award    
The Troy School District has many outsta nding support staff employees. This evening 
we are proud to recognize the accompli shments of nine individuals who were 
nominated for the Troy School District Outstanding Support Staff Award. 
The nominees represent exemplary staff at every level and job description. They were 
nominated by their colleagues , by parents and by former and current students, all of 
whom talked about the impact  these employees have on the lives of those around 
them. We are grateful for their dedication and passion for our students. 
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Distribute 
certificates/plants.) 
Mary Curl, Secretary, Morse Elementary School 
Lois Hurdle, Secretary, TRC/Curriculum & Instruction 
Krishna Patel, ESL Tutor, Smith Middle School 
Becky Murphy, Secretary, Schroeder Elementary School 
Erin Prendergast, Para Educator, Troy Union Elementary School 
Sue Younan, ELL Parent Liaison, Costello, Wass and Troy Union Elementary Schools 
Fran Mahoney, Health Care Aide, Larson Middle School 
Margaret Burchi, Title 1 Tutor, Larson Middle School 
Karen Guilbert, Health Care Aide, Costello Elementary School 
From this outstanding pool of nominees, Mary Curl was awarded the Troy School 
District Outstanding Support Staff Employee of the Year. Congratulations, Mary! 
Mary’s name was also sent to the county for consideration for the county-wide Betty 
Campion Outstanding Support Staff Award.   
Tonight, we are pleased to announce that  Mary was selected as the 2014 winner at 
that level. Dr. Vickie Markavitch, Superintendent of Oakland Schools is here to present 
Mary with that award.   
Dr. Markavitch?  (Dr. Markavitch speaks)

2. RECOGNITION 
B.  Declaration of Special Tribute –  
                                  Dr. Barbara Fowler, Superintendent of Schools 
Though we wish her well, this year the Troy School District will experience a 
tremendous loss with the retirement of Dr. Barbara Fowler.  Dr. Fowler has 
dedicated 31 years of her life to this District, and tonight she is being recognized 
with a Declaration of Special Tribute from the State of Michigan. 
President Philippart will read Declaration.

retiree with a retirement gift (clock).]

                 2. D.     05.06.14 
2.       RECOGNITION 
 D.  National Teacher Appreciation Week 
Teacher Appreciation Week is observed nationally each year in May. This 
year, Teacher Appreciation Week is being celebrated May  5th – May 9th.  At 
tonight’s meeting , the Troy School District and Board of Education would 
like to recognize all of our outstanding teachers by adopting the following 
resolution: 
WHEREAS, throughout Troy and across the country, teachers inspire, 
nurture and open children’s minds to new ideas,  
WHEREAS, teachers promote and strengthen the principles of democratic 
equality, develop leaders and encourage critical thinking; and 
WHEREAS, Troy teachers set the bar for excellence in education and 
continually exceed the highest standards in the profession; and 
WHEREAS, the goal of Teacher Appreciation Week is to raise national and 
local awareness of teaching excellence in building a better society; now,  
THEREFORE BE IT  RESOLVED, that the Troy School District supports 
National Teacher Appreciation Week, May 5-9, 201 4, and encourages our 
schools and community to appropriately recognize the vital and changing 
role of teachers in imagining and creating a better future for society. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution.

  Supported by:

BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
BOARD MEETING   MINUTES   APRIL 1, 2014 
       TROY HIGH SCHOOL  
  President  Philippart  called  the  meeting  to  order  at  7:03 
p.m. 
In  addition  to  Dr.  Philippart,  present  were  Board 
members Edmunds, Hauff Fleming, Miletti, Schmidt, and   
Underwood.   Also  present  were  Superintendent  Fowler; 
administrators Birmingham, Brzezinski, Cottone, DiPonio, 
Keeling,  Lesser,  Machesky,  Mull,  Rajter,  and  Witt;  and 
approximately 200 members of the audience.  
RECOGNITION  
Troy School District’s Outstanding 
Teachers 
The  Board  recognized  all  those  nominated  for  the  Troy 
School  District  Outstanding  Teacher  Award  Program  for 
the  2013/2014  school  year.   The  nominees  represent 
exemplary educators at every level and were nominated 
by  their  peers,  by  parents  and  by  former  and  current 
students,  all  of  whom  talked  about  the  impact  these 
teachers  have  on  the  lives  of  those  around  them.  The 
nominees include: Marianne Belleville (Wass Elementary 
School),  Karen  Bush  (Schroeder  Elementary  School),  
Lauri  Bussell  (Martell  Elementary  School),  Cindy  
Christiansen  (Larson  Middle  School),  Jason  Cichowicz 
(Schroeder  Elementary  School),  Genia  Connell  (Leonard 
Elementary School), Anne  Costello (Baker Middle School), 
Erin Detmer (Leonard Elementary School), Bonnie  Gibson 
(Smith  Middle  School),  Lora  Herbert  (Hill  Elementary 
School),  Stephanie  Hyska  (Boulan  Park  Middle  School), 
Natalie  Jagels  (Smith  Middle  School),  Lindsay  Keegan 
(Schroeder  Elementary  School),  Aaron   Maliepaard 
(Martell  Elementary  School),  Jodi   Martin  (Morse 
Elementary  School),  Mark  Martin  (Boulan  Park  Middle 
School), Grayson  McKinney (Costello Elementary School), 
Trudi Motzenbecker (Wattles Elementary School), Laurie  
Pickell (Martell Elementary School), Kitty Schmidt (Baker 
Middle  School),  and  Amy  Wallace  (Leonard  Elementary 
School). 
Three  of  our  district  nominees  were  sent  to  Oakland 
Schools to represent Troy in the county‐wide outstanding 
9
teacher  award  program.  Due  to  the  criteria  set  by 
Costello  Elementary  was  nominated  at  the  elementary 
level;  Jennifer  Taylor,  Math  Teacher  from  Boulan  Park 
Middle School was nominated at the middle school level; 
and  John  Morrison,  Physics  Teacher  from  Troy  High 
School  was  nominated  at  the  high  school  level.   Mrs. 
Lenihan was also selected as the District’s overall Teacher 
of  the  Year and  was  awarded  a  free  one‐year  lease  of  a 
car,  donated  by  the  Suburban  Collection.   The  Board 
presented  each  nominee  with  a  certificate  and  flowers.  
Mrs.  Lenihan,  Mrs.  Taylor  and  Mr.  Morrison  each  said  a 
few words in appreciation of their nomination.   
Troy Foundation for  
Educational Excellence 
Mini‐Grant Recipients 
This year, the Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence 
was  able  to  fund  90  grants  submitted  by  teachers, 
parents  and  student  groups.   Grant  recipients  were 
recognized for the many diverse ways they are enhancing 
the  education  of  our  Troy  School  District  students.

good turnout.   
Steve  Dunneback,  Royal  Oak  parent,  talked  about  the 
TEA contract and hoped it would be ratified at tonight’s 
meeting.        
School reported on the Athens PTO fundraiser Ford Drive 
4 UR School event, which will be held on Thursday, May

held  on  Thursday  evening  and  that  they  are  hosting  a 
dodge ball event for the Heart Association on April 16th.  
Michaela  also  reported  that  the  Diversity  club’s  annual 
Ethnic Fair is being held on April 23rd at 6:00 p.m. and a 
blood  drive  will  also  be  held  on  that  day.   She  also 
announced  that  Athens  will  be  having  their  4th  Wheel 
Chair  Basketball  game  on  April  25th  to  support  one  of 
their students.

Naven  Karoumy  from  Niles  Community  High  School 
reported  that  their  Environmental  Science  students  will 
begin  test  sampling  at  Firefighter’s  Park  at  the  end  of 
April as part of the River Rouge Project.   He also shared 
that  the  Kiwanis  Club  of  Troy  honored  Naven  Karoumy 
last  week  for  his  success  at  Niles.   He  talked  about  an 
initiative the Niles students are supporting called “Heads 
Up Phones Down”, which is about the dangers of texting 
and  driving.   He  also  mentioned  the  Women  of 
Tomorrow  students  who  met  with  representatives  from 
Kelly  Services.   He  announced  that  five  students  from 
Niles  have  completed  graduation  requirements  during 
the  3rd  quarter  and  will  participate  in  commencement 
ceremonies on June 11th.   
Patty  Durell,  student  representative  from  Troy  High 
School reported that 25 Troy students accompanied two 
teachers  to  Travers  City  for  a  state  student  government 
conference  and  that  principal,  Mr.  Dziatczak  was 
awarded as MASC/MAHS principal of the year.   She also 
reported  that  all  spring  sports  are  in  session  now.   She
shared that Spanish students participated in the National 
Spanish  Camp  and  that  11th  graders  are  beginning  to 
receive their ACT scores. 
Needa  Toofanny,  student  representative  from 
International  Academy‐East,  reported  on  the  HOSA 
(Health  Occupation  Students  of  America)  club  who 
competed  at  the  regional  level  and  that  72  students 
qualified to compete at the state level.   They are hoping 
to  advance  to  the  national  competition.   She  also 
reported  that  the  DECA  state  competition  was  held  last 
month  in  Detroit  and  that  quite  a  few  students  who 
competed  advanced  to  the  national  level.   She  shared 
that  the  Science  Olympiad  team  took  3rd  place  at  the 
regional competition and that all three IA campuses have 
qualified for the state competition.   She announced that 
IA  is  bringing  the  1st  annual  Amazing  Race,  which  is  a 
competition  that  resembles  a  giant  scavenger  hunt.   It 
will take place on April 22nd.  She stated that the last day 
of  school  for  seniors  is  on  April  25th  and  that  the  honor 
societies  are  holding  their  induction  ceremonies  later  in 
April for the graduating class.

FIRST READING –Board Policy  
C‐1402 Smoking 
The  Board  conducted  a  first  reading  of  the  proposed 
changes  to  policy  C‐1402  Smoking.   The  changes  to  this 
policy  were  reviewed  by  the  Board  of  Education’s 
attorney  to  make  sure  it  complies  with  state  law,  the 
Open  Meetings  Act,  and  the  Revised  School  Code.   Ms. 
Underwood read this policy and it will be brought to the 
May  6th  Board  meeting  for  a  second  reading  and 
adoption.

OTHER 
‘Classroom and Kids’ Budget 
Proposal Resolution  
The  Board  approved  the  following  resolution  in  support  of 
the  ‘Classrooms  and  Kids’  budget  proposal,  which  will  be 
sent  to  legislators  regarding  legislative  priorities  and  a 
proposal to: 
Base state funding for education on an accurate assessment 
of  the  cost  to  provide  a  satisfactory  education  to  each 
student,  accounting  for  different  ages,  different  locations, 
and different learning styles and needs. 
Restore Michigan's education system to its past success by 
developing  a  long‐term  plan  to  reverse  the  last  decade's 
cuts in the money available for classroom teaching. 
Maintain  the  integrity  of  K‐12  funding  by  ensuring  that  it 
faces  no  new  revenue  cuts  or  spending  demands  without 
providing matching revenue. 
 RESOLUTION 
IN SUPPORT OF THE 
‘CLASSROOMS AND KIDS’ BUDGET PROPOSAL 
It  was  moved  by  Ms.  Underwood  and  supported  by  Mrs. 
Fleming that   
WHEREAS,  Michigan  public  schools  and  the  children  we 
serve  continue  to  face  difficult  economic  and  budget 
challenges, and 
WHEREAS,  these  challenges  today  have  resulted  in 
circumstances  that  directly  and  negatively  affect  our 
century jobs, and 
WHEREAS,  these  negative  circumstances  range  from  more 
crowded  classrooms,  the  elimination  of  critical  programs 
and reductions in basic supplies, among others, and 
Troy Board of Education Special Meeting 2 April 22, 2014  
24
children’s education, learning and ability to
 compete for 21st 
WHEREAS, these school cuts are directly impacting children, 
parents, teachers, school staff, administrators and the local 
community at large, and 
WHEREAS,  we  recognize  that  the  best  way  to  fix  our  local 
schools and help our children learn is to restore full funding 
WHEREAS,  state‐wide  and  regional  educational 
organizations, including the Michigan Association of School 
Boards,  working  in  partnership  with  parents,  frontline 
educators,  teachers  and  others  have  now  proposed  a 
“Classrooms and Kids” budget that will restore funds to kids 
education, and 
WHEREAS,  the  “Classrooms  and  Kids”  proposal  includes 
providing $250 to $291 per pupil by eliminating $186 million 
in  certain  categoricals,  restoring  more  local  control, 
increasing  at‐risk  funding  and  continuing  the  state’s 
commitment to paying down the MPSERS liability, and 
WHEREAS,  the  “Classrooms  and  Kids”  proposal  provides
stronger funding for kids in their classrooms by redeploying 
existing funds and without raising taxes, now therefore, 
BE  IT  RESOLVED,  Troy  Board  of  Education  supports  the 
“Classrooms and Kids” budget proposal because we strongly 
believe  in  restoring  funds  to  our  classrooms  so  all  children 
have an opportunity to learn and compete for 21st century 
jobs, and that local communities can maintain their voice in 
local issues affecting our public schools, and 
BE  IT  FURTHER  RESOLVED,  that  Troy  Board  of  Education 
calls  on  the  members  of  the  Michigan  Legislature  and  the 
Governor to support and approve the “Classrooms and Kids” 
budget.

Mr. Hauff, Mr. Miletti, Mr. Schmidt, and Ms. Underwood.  Administrators in attendance 
were  Superintendent  Fowler,  Ms.  Birmingham, Mrs.  Keeling,  Dr.  Machesky,  Mrs.  Mull, 
and Mr. Rajter.  There were approximately 5 members of the audience.

Bid Package 9775  
After analyzing the information provided in the proposals from the vendors listed below, I 
am recommending that Aero Filter, Inc. be awarded the bid package for Air Filters at a 
combine cost of $81,964.92 over a two year period:   
 2014 2015 
Aero Filter, Inc.  $  40,982.46 $  40,982.46 
Filter Engineering Corporation  $  45,624.13 $  45,624.13 
Mid-West Air Filter Inc.  $  49,065.27 $  50,537.23 
Tri-Dim Filter Corporation  $  45,468.03 $  45,468.03 
Annually we replace approximately 9,660 filters, which 70% of them are custom made for 
the wide range of makes and models that provide fresh air, heat and/or cooling in our 
facilities.  Filter replacement is a vital part of maintaining the air quality, cleanliness and 
comfort of the buildings.   
If you have any questions, please do not hesitate in contacting my office.  
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department

                            6. A.    05.06.14 
6.       PERSONNEL 
A.      REPORT:  Superintendent Search 
The Troy Board of Education  has been searching for  a superintendent to 
succeed Dr. Barbara Fowler, who is retiring after 31 years  of distinguished 
service to the Troy School District. The Board has been assisted in the search by 
the Michigan Association of School Boards (MASB).   First and second round 
public interviews were held on April 21st and April 29th concluding with site visits 
to both candidates’ districts on May 1 st.  The finalists who are being considered 
for the position of Troy School District superintendent are Dr. Richard Machesky, 
Assistant Superintendent Secondary Instruction of Troy Schools and Dr. Steve 
Matthews, Superintendent of Novi Community Schools.  The Board also met last 
evening in a special meeting to review candidate assessments and site visit 
reports.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION :  President Philippart will provide a background on the 
superintendent search process that has taken place over the last four months.  This 
will also be the opportunity for Board members to share their thoughts and 
perspectives of the candidates selected as finalists for the superintendent position.

     8. A. 5.06.14 
8. BUSINESS 
A.  REPORT:  Proposed Oakland Intermediate School District Budget 
Effective in 2004, Section 624 of the Revised School Code made it a 
requirement for every Intermediate School District to submit a copy of its 
budget to local school districts no later than May 1.  Local districts are 
required to pass a resolution either i n support or disapproval of the 
budget no later than June 1.  Mark Rajter will present the budget to the 
Board. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION: 
This report will be given by Mark Rajter.  No action is required. 
113

Overview of Fiscal Year 2014-
2015
Oakland Schools Proposed Budgets
114
ISD Budget Resolution
Revised School Code 380.624 - Overview
1. Not later than May 1 of each year, the ISD shall submit proposed 
budget for next fiscal year to the board of each constituent district 
for review
2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent 
district shall review the proposed ISD budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or disapproval of the proposed 
budget
3. Mail copy of resolution indicating support or disapproval (with 
rationale) to the Oakland Schools Board of Education

     8. B.    05.06.14 
8. BUSINESS 
 B. CONSIDERATION:  Approve Oakland Intermediate School 
District 2014-2015 Budget 
  In accordance with Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, 
the Troy Board of Education is required to review the proposed Oakland 
Intermediate School District budget and express either its support or 
disapproval of this budget.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
WHEREAS: 
 1. Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, requires the 
intermediate school board to submit its proposed budget no later than May 1 
of each year to the board of each constituent district for review; and 
 2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent district shall 
review the proposed intermediate school district budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or d isapproval of the proposed 
intermediate school district budget, and shall submit to the intermediate 
school board any specific objections and proposed changes the constituent 
district board has to the budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Troy Board of Education has received and reviewed the proposed 
intermediate school district budget in accordance with Section 624 of the 
Revised School Code, as amended, and, by the adoption of this resolution, 
expresses its support for the proposed interm ediate school district budget.  
The Troy School District Board of Education believes that it is the responsibility 
129
of the Intermediate School District to be responsible for its own operations, 
including adoption of its budget.  The Troy School District’s s upport of the 
Oakland Intermediate School District’s budget is for the sole purpose of 
complying with Section 624 of the Revised School Code. 
 2. The secretary of the Troy Board of Education or his/her designee shall forward 
a copy of this resolution to the intermediate school board or its superintendent 
no later than June 1, 2014. 
 3. All resolutions insofar as they conflict with this resolution be and the same are 
hereby rescinded. 
Yes:  
No:  
Resolution declared adopted. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education 
The undersigned duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of Education of the 
Troy School District, Michigan, hereby certifies that the foregoing is a true and 
complete copy of a resolution adopted by the Troy Board of Education at a regular 
meeting held on May 6, 2014, the original of which resolution is part of the Board’s 
minutes, and further certifies that notice of the meeting was given to the public 
under the Open Meetings Act, 1976 PA 267, as amended. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education

Bid Package 9768  
After analyzing the information provided in the proposals, performing background checks 
and determining the best course of action for the District when it comes to maintaining a 
stable operations for both the custodial and grounds operations, I am recommending that 
Enviro-Clean Management Services be awarded bid package 9768.   
Attached are my research notes and concerns when it comes to the companies that provided 
proposals at a lower average cost over the next 3years.  Enviro-Clean Management Services 
annual cost was approximately 4% higher due to the hourly pay rate offered for custodial 
personnel, which has proven to be a benefit to the District because it reduces the change of 
higher turnovers in personnel.   
If you have any questions, please do not hesitate in contacting my office.  
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department  
 Purchasing Department – File  
132
1140 Rankin 
          RE:      Custodial and Grounds Services       
Bid Package 9768 – Custodial and Grounds Services  
GCA Services Group  
 Three Year Average - $2,924,205.54  (Alternate Bid - $3,141,659.53)  
o There is no language in the bid package that could provide clarification for the alternate bid 
increase.   
 Similar Schools  
o GCA has performed in similar sized schools, but does not have a good record with 
maintaining their contracts based on my findings.   
 Salary Information – On average 25% mark up for general labor.   
o Building Leads - $11.00  
 Labor differential from Enviro-Clean base rate of $12.00 per hour is $56,160.00 for 
27 building lead positions.  
o Custodians - $9.50   
 Labor differential from Enviro-Clean base rate of $10.25 per hour is $73,320.00 for 
47 general custodial positions.   
o Utility Crew - $13.00  
 Enviro-Clean rate $14.00 per hour 
o Grounds Crew - $16.00  
 Enviro-Clean rate $13.00 to $15.00 per hour.  
o Warehouse Driver - $12.50  
 Comparable with the other vendors.   
 Management Team – mark up for management team is 49 to 65%.   
o All Salaried Positions  
Summary Note:  GCA does a good job outlining their program for grounds and maintenance, but did not 
provide information that would support or provide information on the benefits of utilizing their custodial 
services.  The red flags for me are; one, the On-Site Manager level of experience with custodial and school 
operations.  The second red flag would be the level of commitment or demand that they have placed on the 
Regional Manager.  I am well aware of his workload and some of the turmoil that GCA has been facing lately 
with keeping proper supervision (leadership) at some of the neighboring school districts.  Mr. Carson is more 
than capable (I know this because I was his supervisor at one time), but he is spread too thin with the number 
of school districts he’s trying to manage.  The final concern would be the custodial operations due to the 
magnitude of the staff and work required.  The level of pay becomes a concern when it comes to keeping 
turnovers to a minimum and making sure the operation is stable.   
Cost different is $128,322.52 between Enviro-Clean and GCA, which comes directly from the hourly labor 
rate offered to building leads and custodians.   
133
Aramark Facility Services  
 Three Year Average - $2,929,488.05 (Alternate Bid - $2,755,818.62) 
o Note:  Aramark does not reflect the correct number of FTE’s as outlined in the bid package 
for Baker Middle School.   
o Alternate Bid would reduce part of the crew from 40hrs/52wks to 40hrs/40wks.  This 
proposal would not cover the basic needs of the District.   
 Similar Schools  
o Based on my research they are only dealing with small school districts (3 to 5 buildings), 
charter schools and academies.   
 Salary Information – Note:  They did not provide the “Charge to TSD per Hour” information.   
o Building Leads - $10.25  
 Labor differential from Enviro-Clean base rate of $12.00 per hour is $98,280.00 for 
27 building lead positions.  
o Custodians - $9.50  
 Labor differential from Enviro-Clean base rate of $10.25 per hour is $73,320.00 for 
47 general custodial positions.   
o Utility Crew - $13.00 
 Enviro-Clean rate is $14.00 per hour.  
o Grounds Crew - $10.00  
 This rate is very low for this type of position.  Enviro-Clean starts at $13.00 per hour.  
o Warehouse Driver - $12.00  
 Comparable with all the other vendors.   
 Management Team – Note:  They did not provide the “Charge to TSD per Hour” information.   
o All hourly employees and the District will be charged overtime for services.  This would 
increase the cost to the overall operations and reduce the savings that may seem to be 
apparent in the individual bid package.  
Sodexo  
 Three Year Average - $2,941,718.00  (Alternate Bid - $2,815,782.00)  
o Alternate would be a reduction in FTE’s during the day and eliminate the Grounds 
Supervisor.  
 Similar Schools  
o Only provided two Michigan based school districts, the rest are all over the United States.  
 Salary Information – On average 45% mark up for general labor.   
o Building Leads - $10.00 to $13.00  
o Custodians - $9.00 to $11.00  
o Utility Crew - $11.00 to $13.00  
o Grounds Crew - $11.00 to $13.00  
o Warehouse Driver - $11.00 to $13.00  
 Management Team – Note:  They did not provide the “Charge to TSD per Hour” information.   
o All Salaried Positions 
Summary Note:  Aramark and Sodexo – Based on my research my concern is that they don’t have the 
practical experience or expertise in the Michigan market to handle the TSD operations.  Their labor rates 
offered for their personnel across the board is a major concern when it comes to maintaining a stable work 
force.

DM Burr

Thoughts and Considerations:  After reviewing and completing my research on each of the companies listed 
about, I am recommending that Troy School District continue utilizing the services of Enviro-Clean Building 
Maintenance Services.  Their performance of the past 5 years has been above average overall and their staff is 
familiar with the facilities, which is crucial as we begin the 2014 Bond Renovations.   I also believe the labor 
rates they offer to their staff helps reduce turnover in personnel, which benefits the District in the long run.   
The additional cost for the increased pay and benefits for TSD past employees will be approximately 
$225,000.00 annually.

D.  CONSIDERATION:  CONTRACTED SERVICES – FOOD SERVICE 
Sodexo Management, Inc. has been providing the food service 
operation for the district since 2009.  Since t he term of the initial 
contract expired, state law requires the District to bid for this service.  
Invitations to bid were sent to 17 potential bidders with 1 responding 
(three of which were “No Bid”).  The response was reviewed by Mr. 
Kenneth Miller, Executive Director. Per bid specs, in no event shall the 
contract be  effective without the prior approval by the Michigan 
Department of Education.  Therefore, the renewal of this contract has 
been submitted to the Michigan Department of Education for 
approval.

 Moved by:   
 Supported by:

Administration to proceed with finalizing an agreement with Sodexo to 
continue to provide Food Services for the 20 14/15 school year, with an 
option to renew for 2015/16, 201 6/17, 201 7/18 and 201 8/19 school 
years; once the approval has been received from the Michigan 
Department of Education.

    RULES AND REGULATIONS    C‐1400 
C‐1402  SMOKINGTOBACCO‐FREE SCHOOLS 
It is the policy of theThe Troy School District that prohibits the use 
and/or  sale  of  any  any  type  of  tobacco  products  and  non‐tobacco 
nicotine  products  (including  smokeless  tobacco  and  any  new 
emerging  tobacco  products)  is  prohibited  in  vehicles,  buildings, 
facilities, structures, or other real estate owned, leased or otherwise 
controlled  by  the  Troy  School  District  and  at  all  school‐sponsored 
events and functions on or off campus, by any person, at any time, 
in  any  location,  and  at  any  event  in  vehicles,  buildings,  facilities, 
structures,  or  other  real  estate  owned,  leased  or  otherwise 
controlled by the Troy School District at all times (24 hours a day – 
7 days a week.) 
For  the  purpose  of  this  policy,  the  use  and/or  sale  of  a  tobacco  or 
non‐tobacco nicotine product is defined as follows: 
 “Tobacco  product”  means  a  preparation  of  tobacco  to  be 
inhaled,  chewed,  or  placed  in  a  person’s  mouth.Tobacco 
products  include,  but  are  not  limited  to,  cigarettes,  cigars,  spit 
tobacco,  shisha  or  hookah  use,  snus,  tobacco  strips,  sticks,  and 
dissolvable  tobacco  products.   Non‐tobacco  nicotine  products 
include,  but  are  not  limited  to,  e‐cigarettes.   This  does  not 
include  products  that  are  classified  as  “nicotine  replacement 
therapy”  and  prescribed  by  a  physician  to  help  tobacco  users 
quit. 
 “Any  person”  includes  students,  employees,  visitors, 
contractors,  delivery  drivers,  etc.,  all  groups  using  school 
property, and any other persons.   
166
 “Any time” means 24 hours per day, seven days per week, and 
365 days per year. 
 “Any  location”  includes  the  school’s  property,  grounds, 
buildings,  and  vehicles,  even  when  school  is  out  of  session  or 
the event is sponsored by another organization. 
 “Any  event”  includes  all  school‐sponsored  events,  whether  on 
or off school property. 
“Use  of  a  tobacco  or  non‐tobacco  nicotine  product”  means  any  of 
the following: 
 The  carrying  by  a  person  of  a  lighted  cigar,  cigarette,  pipe,  or 
  other lighted smoking device. 
 The inhaling or chewing of a tobacco product. 
 The placing of a tobacco product within a person’s mouth. 
Adopted:  February 2, 1999 
Revised:  September, 2003 
  April 20, 2010 
  February 12, 2014 
167

GENERAL SECTION     G-1000 
G-1005 NONDISCRIMINATION 
 In compliance with Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, Title IX of 
the Education Amendments of 1972, Section 504 of the Rehabilitation 
Act of 1973, the Age Discrimination Act of 1975, and the Americans 
with Disabilities Act of 1990, it is the policy of the Troy School 
District that no person shall, on the basis of perceived religion, race, 
color, national origin, age, sex, sexual orientation, disability, height, 
weight, gender identity, marital status, socioeconomic status, or any 
other distinguishing characteristic or association with another person 
who has or is perceived to have any of these characteristics,of race, 
color, religion, national origin or ancestry, gender, age, disability, 
height, weight, or marital status be illegally excluded from 
participation in, be denied the benefits of, or be subjected to 
discrimination during any program, activity, or service or in 
employment. 
 In accordance with Federal regulations, Troy School District has 
appointed the Assistant Superintendent, Human Resources, as the 
Title VI, Title IX, Section 504/ADA, and Age Discrimination Act 
Coordinator.  Any questions, suggestions, or complaints regarding a 
possible or perceived violation of this policy of nondiscrimination 
should be directed to this administrator, who is responsible for 
administering the nondiscrimination grievance procedure: 
Assistant Superintendent, Human Resources 
Title VI, Title IX, Section 504/ADA and Age 
Discrimination Act Coordinator 
Troy School District 
4400 Livernois Road 
Troy, Michigan 48098-4799 
Voice 248.823.4000 
Adopted: July 10, 2007 
Revised:     February 12, 2014

RIGHTS AND RESPONSIBILITIES, CODE OF STUDENT CONDUCT    J‐2000 
J‐2012  ANTI‐BULLYING POLICY 
It is the policy of the District to provide a safe educational environment for all 
students and employees.  Bullying of a student or employee at school, in any 
building within the Troy School District boundaries or any TSD event is strictly 
prohibited. This policy shall be interpreted and enforced to protect all students 
and employees to equally prohibit bullying without regard to its subject matter 
or motivating animus.   
A. Prohibited Conduct.  
1. Bullying.  Bullying of a student or employee at school is strictly prohibited.  
For the purposes of this policy, ʺbullyingʺ shall be defined as: 
Any written, verbal, or physical act, or any electronic communication, that is 
intended or that a reasonable person would know is likely to harm one or more 
students or employees either directly or indirectly by doing any of the 
following:   
a.  Substantially interfering with educational opportunities, benefits, or 
programs of one or more students or employees;  
b.  Adversely affecting a studentʹs or employee’s ability to participate in or 
benefit from  
the Districtʹs educational programs or activities by placing the student or 
employee in reasonable fear of physical harm or by causing substantial 
emotional distress;  
c.  Having an actual and substantial detrimental effect on a student or 
employeeʹs   
  physical or mental health; or 
d.  Causing substantial disruption in, or substantial interference with, the  
  orderly operation of the school.   
2.  Retaliation/False Accusation.  Retaliation or false accusation against a target of 
bullying, anyone reporting bullying, a witness, or another person with reliable 
information about an act of bullying is strictly prohibited.   
171
B.  Reporting an Incident.  If a student, staff member, or other individual believes 
there has been an incident of bullying in violation of this policy, s/he shall promptly 
report such incident to the appropriate principal or designee,  
 C.  Investigation.   All reported allegations of a policy violation or related 
complaint about bullying shall be promptly and thoroughly investigated by the 
building principal or designee.  Each verified incident of bullying and resulting 
consequences including discipline and referrals will be reported annually to the 
school board. 
D.  Notice to Parent/Guardian.  If the principal or designee determines that an 
incident of bullying has occurred, s/he shall promptly provide notification of same to 
the parent/guardian of the victim of the bullying and the parent/guardian of the 
perpetrator of the bullying.   
E.  Annual Reports.  At least annually, the building principal or designee, or the 
Responsible School Official shall report all verified incidents of bullying and the 
resulting consequences, including any disciplinary action or referrals, to the Board of 
Education.   
F.  Responsible School Official.   The superintendent shall be responsible for 
ensuring the proper implementation of this policy throughout the District.  The 
foregoing appointment shall not reduce or eliminate the duties and responsibilities of 
a principal or designee as described in this policy. 
G.  Posting/Publication of Policy.  Notice of this policy will be: (a) annually 
circulated to all students and staff, (b) posted in conspicuous locations in all school 
buildings and departments within the District, (c) annually discussed with students 
and employees, and (d) incorporated into the teacher, student, and parent/guardian 
handbooks. 
H.  Definitions 
1. ʺ At schoolʺ means in a classroom, elsewhere on school premises, on a school 
bus or other school‐related vehicle, or at a school‐sponsored activity or event 
whether or not it is held on school premises.  ʺAt schoolʺ also includes any 
conduct using a telecommunications access device or telecommunications service 
provider that occurs off school premises if the device or provider is owned by or 
under the Districtʹs control. 
2. ʺ Telecommunications Access Deviceʺ means that term as defined in Section 
219a of the Michigan Penal Code, 1931 PA 328, MCL 750.219a, as may be 
amended from time to time.   
172
3. ʺ Telecommunications Service Providerʺ means that term as defined in Section 
219a of the Michigan Penal Code, supra, as may be amended from time to time.   
ADOPTED:   JUNE 5, 2012 
REVISED:   MARCH 6, 2014 
173
